# Multi-Level Work Breakdown Structure (WBS)

> **Companion design document**. This document accompanies
> [RFC-039 Compiler Architecture Refactoring](../../rfc/accepted/039-compiler-architecture.md),
> expanding the RFC's phase sequence into **independently committable and independently verifiable**
> three-level tasks.
>
> RFC-039's main text is the sole authority for phase order and acceptance gates; this document only
> decomposes the work into an executable granularity and records conflicts that have not yet been
> adjudicated.

## Positioning and Scope

| Level       | Count                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Meaning                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| ----------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Level 1** | 11 (P0–P10)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Phases, in one-to-one correspondence with RFC-039's phase sequence                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| **Level 2** | 72 (2026-10-13: 53 +2 = 3.13 / 3.14; 2026-10-14: +1 = 3.15; 2026-10-14: +1 = 3.16; 2026-10-15: +2 = 3.17 / 3.18; 2026-10-16: +1 = 0.4; 2026-10-18: +3 = 3.19 / S7 / S8; 2026-10-18: +2 = 3.21 / S9 (the original registration number for this group was 3.20; it moved to 3.21 on 2026-10-21, and 3.20 was assigned to “pre-existing false positives in dead-code analysis”; see the closing record in [09a](./09a-wbs-history.md)); 2026-10-21: +2 = 0.5 / 3.20; 2026-10-21: +1 = S10 (the S9 number proposed for the 4.4 batch was already used by “pre-release audit abstraction closure,” so it was deferred); 2026-10-24: +1 = 3.22; 2026-10-25: +1 = 3.23; 2026-10-26: +1 = 9.12; 2026-10-27: +1 = S11)                                                                                                                                                     | Task groups, derived from the “Implementation Points” sections of the various documents + the 2026-10-07 audit addendum (P3's 3.4) + the 2026-10-10/11 adjudication supplements (P3's 3.5/3.6/3.7/3.8/3.9) + the 2026-10-12 registration (P3's 3.10) + the 2026-10-12 discovery supplement (P3's 3.11) + the 2026-10-12 discovery follow-up (P3's 3.12) |
| **Level 3** | 196 (2026-10-13: 144 +2 = 3.13.1 / 3.14.1; 2026-10-14: +2 = 3.15.1 / 3.16.1; 2026-10-15: +2 = 3.17.1 / 3.18.1; 2026-10-16: +2 = 4.6.2 / 4.9.6 (`#450`, user adjudication A); 2026-10-16: +1 = 0.4.1; 2026-10-17: +1 = 4.9.7 (`#464`); 2026-10-18: +3 = 3.19.1 / S7.1 / S8.1; 2026-10-18: +12 = 4.9.8 / 3.14.2 / 4.10.4 / 4.10.5 / 3.21.1–3.21.4 (formerly 3.20.1–3.20.4, renumbered on 2026-10-21) / S9.1 / S9.2 / S2.2 / 4.4.4; 2026-10-21: +7 = 3.6.2 / 3.6.3 / 3.6.4 / 0.5.1 / 0.5.2 / 3.20.1 / 3.20.2; 2026-10-21: +1 = S10.1; 2026-10-22: +5 = 3.6.5 / 3.6.6 / 4.10.6 / 4.10.7 / 4.10.8; 2026-10-24: +1 = 3.11.3; 2026-10-24: +1 = 0.5.3; 2026-10-24: +1 = 3.22.1; 2026-10-25: +2 = 3.23.1 / 3.18.2; +2 = 3.20.3 / 3.18.3; 2026-10-26: +1 = 3.20.4; 2026-10-26: +1 = 9.12.1; +1 = 9.12.2; 2026-10-27: +1 = S11.1; 2026-10-31: +3 = 3.20.5 / 3.23.2 / 3.20.6) | Independently committable actions                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |

**Reserved gaps (not reused)**: P4 has no `4.7`/`4.8`, and P8 has no `8.1`. None of these three has
had a corresponding entry since the WBS was established—the entire history of this document has zero
matches for the `**4.7` / `**4.8` / `**8.1` headings
(`git log -S'**4.7' -- docs/src/dev/architecture/09-execution-wbs.md` can verify this), and there
are no repository-wide references. `8.1` corresponds precisely to phase 1 of `05` (test revival and
completion): that phase was split into P1 (`1.1`–`1.3`) and `8.8`, so P8's numbering aligns with its
phase numbers (phase 0 → `8.0`, phase 2 → `8.2`, and so forth).

**Covered by this document**: task decomposition, dependencies, parallelization groups, and
acceptance-criteria bindings.

**Not covered by this document**: the design rationale for tasks (see `02`–`08`) or the rationale
for phase ordering (see RFC-039).

**Registration convention (effective 2026-10-12)**: every newly registered Level 2/3 row **must
contain two fields**—“**Owning stage**” (the P to which it truly belongs, which may differ from the
containing section) and “**Blocks the main line**.” `Pending scheduling` is no longer the default
state; an unmarked task is treated as **not schedulable**. Numbers must be **unique across the
entire document** (cross-track tasks use the track number, such as `029g.x`, and must not occupy an
`N.x` number). **Check-off/completion registration is based on “task id + evidence (gate values, run
id, issue number, file:line),” not commit numbers**—`pull --rebase` rewrites local hashes, so
hard-coded numbers inevitably become invalid (2026-10-13 example: `94ad03ce4`→`631eb3e3c`). Use
`git log` to look up commit numbers.

## Current State: Gaps Found During Decomposition

While consolidating the “Implementation Points” of eight documents into the master table, **five
hard gaps in the documentation system itself** were found. All have now been closed:

| #   | Gap                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Resolution                                                                                                                                                     |
| --- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| G1  | **P5 has no implementation steps**. RFC-039 defines P5 (splitting the checker) and its acceptance criterion (C1 zero-diff), but none of the seven documents describes how to perform the split; `01-routing.md` mentions `annotations.rs` once in its directory tree, the only mention in the entire batch                                    | The P5 steps are supplied here (see below)                                                                                                                     |
| G2  | **The three `include!` refactoring locations have no implementation**. RFC-039 lists it under P4; `02` assigns it to itself; `06` also lists it under S7. Yet `02`'s change list (6 additions + 20 modifications) contains **no `semantic_tokens.rs`**                                                                                        | Implementation steps are assigned here in §P5 5.1 (after P4 is complete); references in RFC-039 and 06 now point here                                          |
| G3  | **Three coexisting lists of CI scripts**. RFC-039 lists four `scripts/ci/*.py`; `01` lists four scripts + 1 `cargo test`; `07` lists four different checks; `04` also requires `check-synth-boundary.py`. After deduplication, there are **at least nine new scripts and no unified list**; `check-obligations.py` is owned by both P4 and P9 | The unified list is in §P9 (11 scripts, each owned by one phase); all other documents refer to this table                                                      |
| G4  | **Conflict over the fate of `precedence_inline.rs`**. `06` counts it among the “1005 lines to revive,” while B6 in the same document says it “must be deleted together with the `Precedence` enum”; `05` advocates deletion                                                                                                                   | **Revive in P1, delete with Pratt in P8** (decision registered as C1)—first restore CI coverage, then delete the file in 8.6 as part of P8's dead-code cleanup |
| G5  | **Conflict in the wording of the L2 goal**. RFC-039 and this directory's index say “grammar-driven syntax,” while `05` self-assesses: “**We did not adopt true grammar-driven parsing (LALRPOP); we only implemented declarative table lookup**”                                                                                              | Converged on **complete LALRPOP grammar-driven parsing** (`05` §Syntax: Complete Grammar-Driven Parsing); G7/G8 acceptance gates enforce that commitment       |

## Target Design: Phase Sequence

```
P0  仓库维护机制与代码放置规程     08        ← 新增，先立规矩
P1  复活孤儿测试并修缺陷           06 §S1
P2  建立等价性判据基线             07 全部
P3  修正确性漏洞（最小方案）        02 §S3-漏洞半部
P4  阶段契约与统一 Driver          02
P5  checker 文件内拆分             02 + 本文补齐
P6  类型表示单一化                 03
P7  中间表示 SSA 化                04      } 可并行
P8  前端范式变更                   05      }
P9  防反弹门禁（含统一脚本清单）     01 §防反弹 + 08 §可机检规则
P10 其余清理与状态修正             06 §S2/S3/S4/S5/S6
```

**Rationale for adding P0**: the three prohibitions—do not invent; do not fill indefinitely; do not
patch when a refactor is required—constrain every action in P1–P10. The rules must be established
before work starts; otherwise, while reviving tests in P1, work would already be adding `mod`
declarations to `tests/mod.rs`, which is precisely what caused the G4 conflict.

Fix = reduce `stderr` to `diagnostics: [[code, file, line], …]` + retain only **crash marker lines** (panic / ICE / backtrace header), and add `strip_ansi` (the run side carries color codes) plus lookahead for positions (when source code is interposed between the diagnostic header and `-->`, the position is still found); `exit` / `stdout` / `check.*` / `expectation` remain unchanged                                                                                                                                                                  | 0.5.1        | The 356-line baseline is regenerated, and `check-corpus-parity` has an empty diff; **rerunning the probe after changing locale text still yields zero diff** (rationale verified empirically); mutation check: changing one `run.exit` to 99 reports one diff; cost = the corpus layer no longer verifies runtime text, which is instead covered by locale unit tests / `yx explain` snapshots / generated-artifact reconciliation; **Owning stage: P0; Blocks the main line: No**                                               |
|                                                            | 0.5.2 Channel wiring and calibration: hook `cargo test` into pre-commit and switch CI to the `fast` (before local commits) and `full` (before push and in CI) dual channels; calibrate the 2600-tier resource profile empirically (currently extrapolated); add resource-marker and exclusive-tier details to `test-specification.md` (**pending scheduling**; follow-up slice of #486)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | 0.5.1        | The pre-commit `cargo test` hook no longer runs the full suite bare (currently `cargo test --all` runs everything; the “Common Commands” section of AGENTS.md already specifies the fast/full dual channels and the red line against bare full-suite `--ignored` runs); CI and pre-commit local runs use the same channel semantics; the 2600-tier marker's peak and duration are measured rather than extrapolated; `test-specification.md` has a corresponding details section; **Owning stage: P0; Blocks the main line: No** |

> **There is no size-baseline task**. The 2026-10-03 decision cancelled all line-count / size gates;
> scale issues are solved through responsibility separation (see `08` prohibition 2, criterion A,
> which calls for human judgment). **0.2.1 is this phase's core acceptance criterion**: if the gate
> cannot report the known parallel representations, the criterion design is wrong and the gate is
> false.

### P3 Correctness Defects (Minimal Solution)

Source: [02](02-stage-contract.md) §Implementation Points S3, defect-fix half + **the 2026-10-07
silent-drop-point audit (3.4)**

> **Semantic-correction routing (D59, user adjudication 2026-10-11)**: if this track or a later
> phase discovers a defect where “the old behavior does not count as success”—**A silent failure / B
> silent mismatch / C undefined behavior and resource loss** (crashes, stack exhaustion, silently
> incorrect answers)—it **may be fixed alongside the refactor**, but all four conditions are
> mandatory: ① red criterion first, and it must genuinely be red; ② no change to the externally
> observable behavior of programs that previously compiled successfully and ran correctly (they must
> not start failing, and their existing diagnostic codes/text/exit codes must not be removed or
> changed); ③ same-commit registration (this row identifies the error class + adds a revision note
> for that phase + the PR's required “Error Type” field); ④ regenerate the baseline in the same
> commit + manually review the diff. **D user-visible semantics / E external-contract table / F
> RFC-level design changes still require prior user adjudication.** See
> [07 §Routing Criteria for Semantic Corrections](07-equivalence-oracle.md) for criterion expansion
> and the A–F whitelist; **“default super-refactor + retroactive registration” is not accepted**
> (rationale in RFC-039 D59: without a zero-red criterion, fixes cannot be distinguished from
> regressions, conflicting with both “C5′ does not exist” and “no relaxations whatsoever”).

>

- [x] **3.22 Decomposition semantics for function-type annotations** (1 Level 3 task, registered
      2026-10-24, issue [#501](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/501); source = follow-up
      from F4 in the 4.10 round-2 batch review: a **parenthesized function type in return position**
      has its level shifted during decomposition—`g: () -> ((x: Int) -> Int) = inc` reports E1012
      and expects `int64` (the annotation is read as **curried**, i.e. “one value parameter
      returning Int”), while `g: () -> ((x: Int) -> Int) = () => { return inc }` expects
      `fn(int64)->int64` but finds the lambda's **overall** type (`fn() -> fn(int64)->int64`); the
      two paths give **mutually contradictory expectations** for the same annotation, so a “function
      whose declared return type is a function” has **no valid annotation form at all** (removing
      the annotation allows it to pass). **The annotation itself parses correctly** (the parser was
      ruled out empirically). **Owning stage: P3; Blocks the main line: No**)

- [x] **3.23 Silent degradation when manifest parsing fails / required fields are missing** (1 Level
      3 task, registered 2026-10-25, issue [#503](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/503);
      source = empirical measurement during WBS 4.9.8 implementation—when `yaoxiang.toml` parsing
      fails (invalid TOML) or a required field such as `[package] version` is missing, the CLI
      **reports no manifest error** and silently degrades to single-file semantics ⇒ the cross-file
      reference pool becomes invalid ⇒ false `W1002`×2 (`a.yx:1:1` / `b.yx:1:1`; both types are
      referenced by the entry point), while `check` still exits 0; the pre-fix and post-4.9.8-fix
      behavior is identical item by item (predates #465 and is unrelated to this fix); D59 **class
      D** (externally observable behavior), semantics pending adjudication (A: error and exit / B:
      continue with a W code / C: preserve current behavior); **Owning stage: P3; Blocks the main
      line: No**)

> **Batch (user adjudication 2026-10-14)**: the four small items 3.13 / 3.14 / 3.15 / 3.16 are
> **highly related** (method binding, resolution, and inference entry points belong to one path;
> 3.16 is another carrier of the same “named argument” criterion—stdlib native exports), and **are
> merged into one team**—the team's scope is broadened from “one Level 2 task” to “a batch of
> related small items”; batch composition, rationale, and deviations must be registered when that
> team closes (record in [09a](./09a-wbs-history.md)).

read as **curried**, i.e. “one value parameter returning Int”), while `g: () -> ((x: Int) -> Int) = () => { return inc }` expects `fn(int64)->int64` but finds the lambda's **overall** type (`fn() -> Terminal criterion (semantics pending adjudication: A: error and exit / B: continue with a W code / C: preserve current behavior, finalized after user adjudication): an unreadable manifest **must not silently degrade**—the manifest problem must be reported explicitly (error exit or W code), and the resulting `W1002` false positives must disappear; red-to-green: missing `version` / invalid TOML, two red criteria (red state = zero manifest diagnostics + two false-positive `W1002` entries); (**closure registration completed 2026-10-30**: implementation `81d86810` (19 files +415/−77)—role_context becomes three-state `RoleContext { surfaces, test_rules, manifest_diagnostics }`: no manifest (NotFound) = legitimate single-file mode unchanged; readable manifest = existing assembly; **present but unreadable** (other I/O errors / invalid TOML / missing required fields) ⇒ new code **E3024** (error severity, message includes the manifest path and TOML details, span_exempt—#324 contract). **Adjudication A** (2026-10-30, with full authority delegated by the user and recorded): treat it like invalid .yx source—report an error and abort; the diagnostic is injected through the RoleClassification arm (Check/Lsp gating, following the E3020 precedent; the MultiFile run channel carries only W codes, so E3024 does not escape there, and the boundary is registered), and the phase is marked failed, so the DeadCodeAnalysis topology that depends on it is skipped ⇒ the degraded W1002 false positive disappears and abortion occurs before degradation; phases that do not depend on it, such as Typecheck, continue running. The new code follows the four-step process (E1043 precedent): code table + code-tables --fix + six locales + generator regeneration. red again**. **Risk**: adding E4018 to the multi-file path is a breaking change.

diagnostic is a behavioral change and requires synchronized corpus-differential baseline and

> only hard prerequisites for 3.1/3.2 are **2.3.2 (multi-file corpus layer) + 2.4.1/2.4.3 (two red
> criteria)**; 2.1/2.2/2.3.1/2.3.3 (IR verifier, snapshots, single-file differential, performance
> baseline) may proceed in parallel with P3. Stopping a correctness defect (`Sorted(3)` silently
> passing) must not be blocked by snapshot infrastructure. 3.3 (panic → diagnostic) has no
> prerequisites and may land at any time.

### External Track RFC-029g: Remove `pub` and Automatic Binding (External Track / #399 — Ordering Constraint D56)

Source: [RFC-029g](../../rfc/accepted/029g-remove-pub-and-auto-bind.md) (accepted 2026-10-02,
implementation not started), [#399](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/399). **This track
is not a design item of the refactor itself; it is a specification-conformance deletion forced by an
ordering constraint**; it is distinct from P10 S5 “reduce the visibility of `pub` items” (narrowing
Rust's `pub(crate)`).

**Why it must precede P4 as a whole**: 029g's deletion surface overlaps the rewrite surface of all
four refactoring phases; reversing the order means redoing the same deletions and baseline updates
on the new grammar / new checker layout / new SSA IR.

| 029g deletion surface                                               | Files                                                                                                              | Rewritten by which phase                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `KwPub` and syntax branches                                         | `lexer/{tokens,state,mod}.rs`; `parser/parser_state.rs`, `statements/{declarations,imports,functions,bindings}.rs` | **P8** (8.2 lexical convergence / 8.3 grammar construction / 8.6 Pratt deletion / 8.7 splitting `parse_assign_after_target`)                                                                                                                                                      |
| AST `is_pub` ×2 (`Assign` / `TypeDefinition`)                       | `parser/ast.rs:247/270`                                                                                            | **P6 6.5 AST dead variants** + P8 + D1 directory renaming (`parser/ast.rs` → top-level `src/ast/`)                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `auto_bind_to_type` and the pub branch of `collect_exports`         | `typecheck/checker.rs:2150/3802/3836`, `environment.rs:284`                                                        | **P5** (`include!` → real `mod` and checker splitting are **pure relocation**, so the pub branches are moved unchanged into the new files)                                                                                                                                        |
| Dead-code exemptions `exempt_pub` / `is_exported` and role dispatch | `typecheck/passes/dead_code.rs`, `module/orchestrator.rs:389-407` (inside `check_project`)                         | **P4 4.2.2** (`check_project` slimming)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `semantic_tokens`' `Public` modifier                                | `typecheck/checker/semantic_tokens.rs:450/498`                                                                     | **P4 4.2.6** + P5 + 4.6 (D55)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `ir_gen`'s `is_pub`-ignoring binding                                | `middle/core/ir_gen.rs:1378`                                                                                       | **P7** (`ir_gen.rs` SSA conversion)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| 31 occurrences of `pub` under `src/std/`                            | `std/list.yx`(25) / `json.yx`(4) / `option.yx`(1) / `result.yx`(1)                                                 | No phase changes these directly, but **P6's hard acceptance criterion requires zero differences under `src/std/` (`git diff --stat src/std/` is empty; direct-driver tests under `tests/` are exempt as described in P6's hard-acceptance section)**—they can only land before P6 |

**Incidental benefit**: 029g deletes the dead `env.exports` table (populated in production, with
zero repository-wide consumers) and the `is_exported`/`is_visible` accessors ⇒ one fewer “produced
but never consumed” field in P4's 4.3 obligation ledger.

**Acceptance (behavioral changes, red criteria first)**:

1. **Red-to-green**: each of the two behavioral changes must have a red criterion before code
   changes—① `pub` changes from “silently accepted” to a parse error; ② unreferenced top-level
   bindings in Lib/Script roles newly report W1001 (pub items were previously absolutely exempt).
2. **Test count must not decrease**: when deleting the `exempt_pub` case in
   `typecheck/passes/tests/dead_code.rs` and the `is_pub` fixture, equivalent new cases must replace
   them.
3. **Baseline in the same commit**: diagnostic-set changes (a new parse error + new W1001) ⇒ the
   corpus differential baseline (`tests/baselines/corpus-parity.jsonl`) and CHANGELOG are updated in
   the same commit; IR snapshots are expected to have zero diff, and any non-zero diff requires an
   explanation.
4. **Independent revert unit**: the entire batch occupies one commit group and must not be mixed
   with other phases (DoD 6).
5. `cargo test` / `clippy -D warnings` / `python scripts/rfc/check_tracking.py` are all green.

**Ordering**: overlaps with 3.4 in files (`checker.rs`, `environment.rs`) ⇒ the two must be serial
and cannot run in parallel; **the whole track precedes P4** (RFC-039 D56). The 029g track does not
depend on 3.4; the implementer chooses which to do first based on available context.

> **Commit cross-reference (2026-10-08)**: this section's incorporation (the P3.5 chapter +
> main-line / constraint rows) was included in `97071c81b` together with the RFC-039 D56 cleanup
> during a parallel-stream workspace cleanup—the commit message records “3.4 addendum registration +
> D57/D58 adjudication filing” and does not mention this track. History is not rewritten; following
> the existing style (the `d5b18390` precedent, see the P2 landing note), it is registered in place
> as retrospective binary-search evidence.

4.10.4 has already made the order within a cycle **deterministic** (Tarjan SCC contraction + path lexicographic order within the cycle), but the “cycle” itself is still silently tolerated. **User adjudication A on 2026-10-23: compile-time W-code warning—a cycle must not be silent** (reading an uninitialized top-level value within a cycle must have a visible signal); **three points pending deliberation, finalized 2026-10-24**: ① warning target = “**a cycle exists**” (a static fact including the cycle path)—“reading an uninitialized value within a cycle” does **not** get a separate code, but is merged as **precise information into the same code's help** (the cross-unit read set can be obtained in one pass over the merged IR's init section, as empirically verified); ② do **not** add an `#[allow]`-style suppression mechanism (the existing output is W by default, non-blocking + `check --deny-warnings` upgrades it); ③ register at the “**certain fact**” level, on par with W1001–W1006 (the code table has only one `span_exempt` boolean and RFC-013 has no “evidence level” table, so this is a documentation convention rather than a data-structure change); **completed 2026-10-24** (commit `28c70a518`): added **W1007**—detection reuses 4.10.4's Tarjan SCC (`src/driver/helpers.rs` returns `InitOrder{order, import_cycles}`; no second cycle detector), and the Linking arm emits one diagnostic per cycle anchored to a real `use` statement within the cycle, using the existing `check_diagnostics` channel; value-edge information (read side `Load{src: Global}` precedes the slot's first `Store{dst: Global}` and crosses units) is included in help, naming the violated edge and the qualified name of the read slot; the four-part set is committed together (code table / six locales / RFC-013 W1xxx table / generated documentation); the `main → zlib → zother → zlib` fixture empirically produces check exit 0 with **exactly one** W1007, while run still exits 1 and retains E6007 (**exit code unchanged**), and `check --deny-warnings` exits 1; measured corpus false-positive surface **0** (at t1's measurement point: 385 `.yx` files / 82 edges / 0 multi-member SCCs / 0 self-loops; after adding eight fixtures, `HEAD` contains 397 `.yx` files—independent verification (t5) measured zero W1007 occurrences across five acyclic fixtures on the frozen tree, and review (t6) separately measured that a benign cycle reports exactly one W1007 while runtime exit remains 0); E6007's misleading text is **not changed** (a separate case) | 4.10.4        | **Terminal criterion**: an import cycle no longer uses E6007 “type mismatch” to direct users toward a type error, and **must not be silent**—the cycle form produces a compile-time W-code diagnostic (warning surface finalized by the three points above); **Owning stage: P4; Blocks the main line: No**                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
|                                            | 4.10.7 **Native functions as values** (issue [#492](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/492)): `use std.io.{println}` + top-level `f = println` + `f("hello")` currently gives **exit 0, no output, and no diagnostic** (3.6.5's fix deliberately does not cover native functions and remains consistent with pre-fix behavior). **User adjudication B on 2026-10-23: genuinely support it**—**materialize** the native function **as a value**, **without adding a diagnostic code**; **terminal state**: the same form must **actually print hello**; **completed 2026-10-24** (commit `19deb87d3`): the single representation takes the **constant form**—`ConstValue::NativeFn(qualified name)` (constant-pool tag 7) + runtime `RuntimeValue::NativeFunction`; the single criterion source `ModuleRegistry::is_native_fn_export` = `is_native_name` ∧ ¬`is_yx_std_export` ∧ category Function (reusing 4.10.8's pure-yx criterion without writing another name determination); emission converges on one place, `ir_gen::emit_module_export_value`, and **all three entry points share it** (`use lib.{f}` alias / `lib.f` namespace member / module variable member `io.println`—the member path previously also did nothing silently and is fixed together); `f = println` + `f("hello")` actually prints; cases cover parameters / multiple parameters / return values / use as an argument / second-order passing; native constants used as values and both direct-call forms have **zero drift**                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | 3.6.5         | **Terminal criterion**: `use std.io.{println}` + `f = println` + `f("hello")` has hello in stdout and exit code 0; the native-function-as-value surface (with parameters / multiple parameters / return values) is also defined; std controls (native constants used as values) remain unchanged; **Owning stage: P4; Blocks the main line: No**                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
|                                            | 4.10.8 **Pure-YaoXiang std exports as values** (issue [#493](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/493)): `std.test` / `std.list` are **pure yx functions** (not native), but were excluded from 3.6.5's repair by the blanket rule “module origin = std” (the criterion `alias_targets_user_function` excludes `ModuleSource::Std`), so `use std.list.{f}` + top-level rebinding **could** be silent like #479 (review marks it medium, **not verified**—empirical confirmation is required); **user adjudication on 2026-10-23: class C, no diagnostic code, directly fixable**; **completed 2026-10-24** (commit `5a2fa8829`): the criterion changes from the blanket “module origin = `ModuleSource::Std`” to **export granularity**—the set of pure-yx export names in `ModuleInfo` (its sole write point is where embedded sources are merged in `register_std_modules`, with data from `src/std/yx_sources.rs`) + `ModuleRegistry::is_yx_std_export`; `alias_targets_user_function` is renamed `alias_targets_code_function` (this criterion function was renamed a second time to `qualified_export_is_code` in #492 commit `19deb87d3`, at `src/middle/core/ir_gen.rs:817`, with consumer `:860`; the value-position criterion and emission point `emit_module_export_value` are combined into one place); t1 empirically **split the original hypothesis into two forms**: a non-generic pure-yx export used as a value previously **silently** returned `unknown(void)` / exit 0, while a generic `std.list.map` previously produced runtime E6007—both terminal states are fixed; mixed surfaces such as `std.result` (native + pure yx) are evaluated item by item by name; native exports still use zero-argument `Call` (handled by 4.10.7)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | 3.6.5         | **Terminal criterion**: pure-yx std exports used as values follow the same form and criterion as user-module exports used as values (no silence); **Owning stage: P4; Blocks the main line: No**                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |

### P5 Splitting the Checker into Files — Supplied Here (Original Gap G1)

Source: RFC definition + the directory tree in `01-routing.md` + the hard constraint (the
`pub(crate)` path of `collect_used_in_type` must not change)

- [x] **5.1 `include!` → real `mod`** (2 Level 3 tasks; completed 2026-10-26)
- [x] **5.2 Extract `refinement`** (2 Level 3 tasks; completed 2026-10-26)
- [x] **5.3 Extract `annotations`** (2 Level 3 tasks; completed 2026-10-26)

| Level 2                     | Level 3                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Prerequisite | Acceptance                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| --------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 5.1 `include!` → real `mod` | 5.1.1 Change the `include!` at the end of `checker.rs` to a real `mod` declaration (do not hard-code the line anchor; it was empirically at the end of the file)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | P4 complete  | C1 zero-diff; `semantic_tokens.rs` gains module identity                                                                                                                                                                                              |
|                             | 5.1.2 Add `use super::*` or item-by-item imports (`include!` implicitly inherited the scope)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | 5.1.1        | `cargo build` is green                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| 5.2 Extract `refinement`    | 5.2.1 Move out the refinement blocks (three non-contiguous regions of the original `checker.rs`; now one `checker/refinement.rs` file containing `ReturnRefinement` / `RefinedWalkCtx`), leaving the shared surface and six cross-boundary shared items in place                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | 5.1.1        | C1 zero-diff                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
|                             | 5.2.2 Refinement entry points converge on “`&self` + return diagnostics,” **without leaving impl**: `collect_refined_binding_checks` (now `checker/refinement.rs`, `pub(super)`) no longer uses `&mut self` to write implicitly to the error channel; the diagnostic set is returned and propagated upward, with the sole write point in the sole caller `check_module_impl` (`checker.rs`, symbol location). **Reason for remaining in impl**: this family's field surface consists only of `self.env` (technically it could leave impl), but converting the entry point and traversal entirely to free functions would move these call sites: `resolve_type_annotation` has eight production sites (six annotation paths + `refined_walk_stmt` + `collect_refined_var_names_in_stmt`), `collect_param_refinements` has two production sites + one test site, and it would create a reverse dependency “retained surface (signature collection) → refinement,” contrary to the coupling this step is intended to remove | 5.2.1        | C1 zero-diff; four **symbol criteria**: ① the signature contains no `&mut self`; ② the function body contains zero `env.errors`; ③ the **returned** diagnostics have exactly one write point, in the caller; ④ there is still exactly one entry point |
| 5.3 Extract `annotations`   | 5.3.1 Move out the 10-member annotation-validation family (now one `checker/annotations.rs` file; `check_annotation_type_names` is the `pub(super)` entry point, the other nine are private to the family, and `is_predicate_head` is a free function within the family)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | 5.2.2        | C1 zero-diff                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
|                             | 5.3.2 **Keep `collect_used_in_type` (`pub(crate)`) as `pub(crate)` and under the `checker` module path**: it is a **shared-surface** function (consumed by both `collect_function_signature` and `inference/statements.rs:16`), and 5.3.1 **did not move it** according to empirical measurement ⇒ the “zero caller changes” criterion **was already satisfied**, so **do not add** a `pub(crate) use annotations::*;` re-export. **Constraint retained**: if it is moved with the family in the future (into annotations or another later batch), `checker.rs` must add `pub(crate) use annotations::*;` so that the import remains unchanged                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | 5.3.1        | **Zero caller changes** is this step's hard acceptance criterion: `git diff src/frontend/core/typecheck/inference/statements.rs` is empty                                                                                                             |

**Hard constraint**: `collect_used_in_type` is used across modules by `inference/statements.rs:16`.
**If** the function is moved with the annotation family in the future, **then** `checker.rs` must
add `pub(crate) use annotations::*;` so that the import remains unchanged; 5.3 did not move it
according to empirical measurement, so this batch added no re-export (the criterion was already
satisfied).

**Only split confirmed large blocks.** Do not split modules that are only 200–400 lines—this
increases navigation cost, reduces cohesion, and yields zero decoupling benefit. The remaining
submodules (signatures / type_defs / imports) wait until the files grow back to 1,500 lines.

**Hard acceptance** (**2026-10-27 revision, carrying forward the 2026-10-26 adjudication ②**):
`git diff --stat tests/yaoxiang tests/baselines src/std/` must be **empty in every round from 6.1
through 6.7**. **Direct-driver test files** under `tests/` (tests that directly exercise changed
code, such as `tests/formatter_integration.rs`) **may** be changed when mechanically required, but
each change must be registered with **file / line count / reason**; **tests must not be deleted and
assertions must not be relaxed**. **Exception**: 6.8.2 is a **behavioral-fix** batch (new diagnostic
code + baseline update), does not fall under this hard acceptance criterion, and is run as a
separate, separately adjudicated round.

> **Revision relationship**: the original wording (2026-10-26 adjudication ②) was
> `git diff --stat tests/ src/std/`, applying a blanket zero-diff requirement to all of `tests/`;
> the revised wording **changes only direct-driver test files from “zero diff” to “change permitted
> but file / line count / reason must be registered”**, while the zero-diff requirement for the
> **corpus layer (`tests/yaoxiang`), baseline layer (`tests/baselines`), and standard library
> (`src/std/`)** remains unchanged. First application = 6.1.2: only `tests/formatter_integration.rs`
> changed, by **3 lines** (two `assert_format_eq` occurrences of `i64`→`Int64` + one comment line),
> because the changed spelling requires the assertion's expected type name to be synchronized; no
> tests were deleted and no assertions were relaxed. See 09a “P6 6.1 Name Normalization Closure.”

switch by C5′. If any of 8.6's approximately 89 BP references are missed, **associativity silently
changes** (no error, only a different parse result).

### P9 Anti-regression Gates (Unified Script List — Original Gap G3 Supplied Here)

Source: [01](01-routing.md) §Anti-regression Mechanism + [08](08-maintenance-mechanism.md)
§Machine-Checkable Rules

**This is the sole authoritative list of all CI scripts** (11 scripts). Scripts are connected to CI
at their “introduction phase”; report-only scripts are converted to hard failures in P9 (changing
“report” to “fail”). Existing violations (parallel representations, disambiguation aliases, etc.)
are cleared only in P6/P8; converting them to hard failures earlier would leave CI red for a long
time and cause gates to be bypassed manually. When P9 converts them to hard failures, the gates
should be exactly green. P2's 2.5, P4's 4.3.2, and P7's 7c.1 refer to this table and do not define
separate lists.

- [ ] **9.1–9.11 Connect eleven scripts at their introduction phases and convert them all to hard
      failures in P9** (details in the table below)
- [x] **9.12 Prerequisite for hard conversion: rebaseline criterion-C connection points and clear
      the stale inventory** (2 Level 3 tasks, **registered and closed 2026-10-26**): the title used
      at registration—“clear existing same-name multiple definitions”—was a **mislabel**—the
      `--strict` violations come only from criterion C (entry-point wiring); criterion A (same-name
      definitions, 181 entries) remains report-only and never makes `--strict` red. Root cause:
      `STAGE_MARKERS` was calibrated on 2026-10-06 against the pre-P4 state where “six paths each
      wired manually”; its comment explicitly says “after P4 unifies the Driver, this table is
      deleted with the inventory,” but after P4 landed, neither the vocabulary nor the 19
      `KNOWN_WIRING_SITES` entries were retired, so the criterion classified the Driver's **own
      phase table** (`Stage::DeadCodeAnalysis` variant name, `slug` string table, `state.rs`
      dependency table) as a violation—the exact opposite of prohibition 3, criterion C's rule text
      (“adding the Nth entry point **instead of registering it in the declarative phase table** →
      violation”). Disposition = **rebaseline the criterion** rather than adding a whitelist.
      Result: `--strict` violations 9 → 0, inventory 19 → **7**, measured connection points 18 →
      **8** (7 inventory entries + 1 dispatch point), criterion-A same-name definitions 181 → 180
      (deleting `build_registry`'s same-name entry), `pub(crate)` 167 → 166. **Owning stage: P9;
      Blocks the main line: No (blocks P9's hard conversion).**
  - 9.12.1 Rebaseline delivery (four commits, itemized in the closing record in
    [09a](./09a-wbs-history.md)): ① **rebaseline the criterion**—LEX merged into `Parsing`
    (`src/driver/stage.rs` and 02 §Target Design 1 both define `Parsing` as “lexical analysis +
    syntax analysis (lex → parse)”), DEADCODE/MONO recognize only call forms, `#[cfg(test)] mod` is
    exempted by **brace pairing**, `dispatch` is separated as `DECLARED_DISPATCH_POINTS`, and 13
    stale inventory entries are cleared (each with an itemized removal reason, empirically
    classified into three groups: 5 deleted functions / 5 functions that remain but no longer have a
    phase primitive / 3 entries reduced to a single phase solely because LEX was merged); ② **derive
    arm forms**—the 12 variants' `self.<arm>(` forms are derived from `dispatch`'s exhaustive match
    (`Stage::ALL` supplies the variants, as a single source) + three fail-loud guards (variant
    source invalid / arm not found / arm has no `fn` definition), and fixes a false positive caused
    by including the signature line in the measured body, which made `fn check_project(` self-match
    TC (the `fn_body` cuts off the signature section; the measured reduction of “≥2 → <2 phases” is
    0); ③ **turn coverage declarations into facts**—the phase implementations of 4 variants
    (VendorConsistency / Discovery / Registry / RoleClassification) are shared primitives outside
    the Driver, and are registered truthfully as known blind spots; ④ **describe blind spots by
    nature**—live wiring (`collect_package_refs`, called at `driver/arms.rs:626`) and dead-code
    candidates (`build_registry`, zero callers) cannot receive the same disposition. Three
    independent reviews all found needs_revision (medium → medium → low; all three findings were
    genuinely fixed), and the fourth round **passed** with no findings. **Acceptance**:
    `python scripts/ci/check-fanout.py --strict` exit 0 (violations 0 / inventory 6 / dispatch
    point 1) ✅
  - 9.12.2 Expand criterion-C coverage and clear `build_registry` (**completed, closed 2026-10-26**;
    user adjudication **D1-A retain + D2-A delete**): ① **delete** the zero-caller chain
    `build_registry` / `build_registry_from` (formerly `orchestrator.rs:379-396`) and the
    now-uniquely-uncalled `discover` (formerly `:416-418`), while retaining `DiscoveredFile` /
    `Discovery` / `extract_module_info` / `parse_file`; before deletion, `build_registry` was
    separately adjudicated as an external API-surface change because
    `src/lib.rs:27 pub mod frontend` → `frontend/module/mod.rs:20 pub mod orchestrator` is a
    lib-target public item. ② **retain** phase primitives outside the Driver that still exist after
    deletion: VendorConsistency ← `ensure_vendor_consistency`, Discovery ← `discover_with_used`,
    RoleClassification ← `roles::classify` (narrowed to a qualified form—a bare name would
    mistakenly catch same-name helpers in other modules; a constructed example reproduces this),
    Registry ← `extract_module_info` (without using bare `.register(`, which occurs under multiple
    names in the repository). ③ Exactly one newly exposed site: `collect_package_refs`
    (`driver/helpers.rs:1057`, live code, called by `driver/arms.rs:626`) is registered, taking the
    inventory from **6 → 7** and measured connection points from 7 → 8. ④ The criterion docstring's
    coverage declaration becomes fact-based: the old “6+6” split is merged into **10+2**, R1–R3's
    determinable inclusion criteria are added, “any function constructing data for that phase is not
    included” is deleted because five table entries disprove it, and three stale line numbers /
    untested figures / two inaccurate comments are fixed (`orchestrator.rs:7` module header,
    `statements.rs:737` reference to the deleted function). Behavior-neutral: corpus 356 rows
    byte-identical, 216 snapshots zero drift, `cargo test --lib` 2855/0/2, and `pub(crate)` 167 →
    166 (an inevitable result of deleting `discover`). Review chain: round 1 needs_revision (one
    medium + two low) → fixes → round 2 **passed** (independently reproduced with a self-built
    probe); then the F4/F5/F6/F8 cleanup round → narrow review **passed** (AST
    statement-by-statement comparison confirms the criterion has no collateral changes). **Owning
    stage: P9; Blocks the main line: No.**

> on 2026-10-03). Scale issues are handled by `08` prohibition 2, criterion A (responsibility

## Implementation Points: Dependencies and Parallelization

### Mandatory Serial Main Line

`P0 → P1 → P2 → P3 → P3.5 → P4 → P5 → P6 → (P7 ∥ P8) → P9 → P10`

> **P3 stop-the-bleeding path (adjudication 2026-10-05)**: P3's prerequisites are narrowed to
> **2.3.2 + 2.4.1 + 2.4.3** (multi-file corpus layer + two red criteria). The rest of P2 (2.1 IR
> verifier, 2.2 snapshots, 2.3.1/2.3.3 differential testing and performance baseline) may proceed in
> parallel with P3. Stopping a correctness defect is not blocked by criterion infrastructure;
> however, **P4 must still wait for all of P2**—the unified Driver needs all three layers of
> criteria in place.

| Constraint                | Reason                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| ------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| P0 → P1                   | The three prohibitions constrain every P1 action (1.2.x is precisely the act of adding `mod` declarations to `mod.rs`)                                                                                                          |
| P1 → P2                   | Reviving tests changes the test count and corpus baseline                                                                                                                                                                       |
| P2 (after narrowing) → P3 | Vulnerability criteria must be **written in red first** (2.4.1/2.4.3) + the multi-file corpus layer (2.3.2) must exist so the red tests have somewhere to run                                                                   |
| P3 → P4                   | Fix bugs before refactoring; the reverse order causes bugs to be frozen into the phase table as “established behavior”                                                                                                          |
| P3.5 → P4                 | RFC-029g (#399)'s deletion surface covers the rewrite targets of P4 4.2.2 / P5 / P6 / P7 / P8 (list in §P3.5 here)—the reverse order means redoing the same batch of deletions on the new grammar, checker layout, and IR (D56) |
| P4 → P5                   | The `include!` refactoring and checker splitting modify the same file                                                                                                                                                           |
| P5 → P6                   | Consecutive changes to the same file must be separated, or regressions cannot be bisected                                                                                                                                       |
| P6 → P7 / P8              | If type representations do not converge first, the new IR grows into a third representation                                                                                                                                     |
| P7/P8 → P9                | The snapshot baseline in 9.5 and the `pub(crate)` leak count in 9.3 reach their final form only after P7/P8                                                                                                                     |

### Parallelization Groups

| Group                                    | Members                                                                            | Basis                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| ---------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **A (the only group authorized by RFC)** | P7 ∥ P8                                                                            | Disjoint file sets: `ir.rs`/`ir_gen.rs`/`bytecode.rs`/`translator.rs` vs `lexer/*`/`parser/*`                                                                                                                                                            |
| B                                        | 1.2.1 ∥ 1.2.2 ∥ 1.2.3 ∥ 1.2.4                                                      | Each independently adds a `mod`; revert file by file                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| C                                        | 2.1 ∥ 2.2 ∥ 2.3.1 ∥ 2.3.2 ∥ 2.4                                                    | The three layers of criteria are independent                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| D                                        | 4.4 (proof-layer completion) ∥ the entire P6 line                                  | `02` explicitly states in S5 that this “should proceed independently of S1-S4”                                                                                                                                                                           |
| E                                        | 4.2.5 ∥ 4.2.6 ∥ 4.2.8                                                              | All are independent after 4.1 is complete                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| F                                        | 9.3 ∥ 9.9 ∥ 9.10 (the three scripts introduced by P0 may be developed in parallel) | The snapshot baseline in 9.5 must be taken last                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| G                                        | S3 ∥ S4 ∥ S5                                                                       | Disjoint file sets (verified 2026-10-05: S3 modifies `docs/src/dev/design/check/` + `config.js`; S4 modifies `docs/src/rfc/` + the `check_tracking.py` generator; S5 modifies two `pub` sites under `src/frontend/core/typecheck/`; no pairwise overlap) |

**Cannot run in parallel (file overlap)**: P1(1.1.x) ∥ P8(8.0.x); 1.2.1 ∥ 8.3.2; 6.0 ∥ S2 (B6/E5 use
the same files); 4.3.2 ∥ 9.2 (now unified under P4); **3.4 ∥ P3.5** (`checker.rs` / `environment.rs`
are the same files—the external track's pub-deletion batch and the silent-path fixes must be
serial).

**New internal-document constraint**: 6.0→6.1→6.2→6.3→6.4→6.5→6.6 is strictly serial; 7a→7b→7c→7d is
strictly serial, and 7b's `Phi` arm must precede 7d.

## Key Decisions and Rationale

- **P0 is an independent phase rather than part of P9**—gates are “post-hoc checks”; rules are
  “pre-hoc judgment.” P9's scripts cannot prevent “patching when a refactor is required”; only
  D0–D4's review checklist can.
- **P9 is the sole authoritative list of CI scripts**—the three coexisting lists behind original gap
  G3 made closure impossible. This table merges 11 scripts, each owned by one phase, with
  report-only status and hard-conversion timing explicitly traceable.
- **P3.5 incorporates RFC-029g rather than leaving it as an “independent track to execute after the
  refactor”**—its deletion surface overlaps the rewrite surfaces of P4/P5/P6/P7/P8 (RFC-039 D56).
  Deferring it until after the refactor means redoing the same deletions on the new grammar, checker
  layout, and IR; this is the same lesson as incorporating `2315`/`2341` into 7e and RFC status
  corrections into S4.
- **P5's steps are supplied here rather than merged into P4**—P4 is already the largest single-point
  risk, and adding checker splitting would exceed the granularity that can be rolled back.
- **5.3.2 uses “zero caller changes” as its hard acceptance criterion**—the split must be
  independently revertible.

## Known Limitations and Risks

- **1.3.x (fixing exposed defects) has no regression criterion**—it only says “fixed defects must
  not be reverted,” without requiring a record of what was fixed. **Execution convention**: every
  1.3.x fix's commit message must cite the fixed test name and defect description as retrospective
  binary-search evidence.
- ~~**7b.1 granularity is too coarse**~~ **Fixed 2026-10-05**: split into exclusive commits 7b.1a /
  7b.1b under DoD 6.
- **8.2.1 granularity is too coarse**—about 89 reference renames; a missed change **silently changes
  associativity**.
- ~~**D17 / D20 have no corresponding Level 3 tasks**~~ **Fixed 2026-10-05**: D17 lands as 7e.1, D20
  as 4.5.1/4.5.2; D32/D34 opcode reclamation is merged into 7e.1, and deletion remains in P10 S6.
- ~~**The `2315` / `2341` data-loss defects were originally “independent issues”**~~ **Fixed
  2026-10-05**: incorporated as 7e.2 / 7e.3. The exception table (throw/try) is core language
  semantics and must not be left as an implementation residual.
- ~~**E2 (`lib.rs:568/582` `todo!()` has no phase ownership**~~ **Fixed 2026-10-05**: merged into
  P10 S2.
- ~~**F7 (opcode-gate extractor) ownership is ambiguous**~~ **Fixed 2026-10-05**: merged into the
  generation-time gate task in P10 S6.

## Conflict Registry: All Adjudicated

> **The original “Conflict Registry” C1–C8 and all open questions in the other documents have
> converged into the [RFC-039 Decision Registry](../../rfc/accepted/039-compiler-architecture.md)
> (D1–D50).** No pending items remain in this document. Itemized correspondence:

| Original Number | Issue                                                                          | Decision                                                                                                                                          |
| --------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| C1              | `precedence_inline.rs` is revived in P1 and deleted in P8                      | **Revive** (P1's premise is to revive tests with real assertions); P8's 8.6 deletes the file together with Pratt                                  |
| C2              | “Grammar-driven” commitment vs table-driven implementation                     | **Fully implemented**: use LALRPOP; see `05` “Syntax: Complete Grammar-Driven Parsing”                                                            |
| C3              | Conflict between C6 category definition and S1 classification                  | **S1 belongs to C6** (revival does not require equivalence criteria, only regression tests); C6 is expanded to “pure deletion or pure revival”    |
| C4              | `test_release_plan_spans_consumed` whitelist                                   | **No whitelist**; the set difference must be empty; if it cannot be empty, the `ReleasePlan` contract is defective → change to `PlanId` (D20/D41) |
| C5              | Internal differences between the two `Aggregation` functions were not verified | **P4's 4.2.7 explicitly lists this as a mandatory verification item**; verify line by line during implementation                                  |
| C6              | The wasm playground fix requires P4's `ProgramKind`                            | **The wasm-path fix is merged into 4.2.8**; P3 handles only the three native entry points                                                         |
| C7              | Ownership of `method_def_ordinals`                                             | **Belongs to 04** (P7 batch c); see D21                                                                                                           |
| C8              | `upvalue_count` correction and the `.42` version number                        | **Fix + change `VERSION` from 4 to 5** (the format header already has `MAGIC` + `VERSION` fields, `codegen/bytecode.rs:14-16`); see D17           |
